The company develops the Braintree Medical Workflow (BMW) Platform, an integrated software suite designed primarily for outpatient procedure centers, ambulatory surgery centers, vascular access centers, and specialty clinics. The company also operates under the name \"NephroPACS\" (nephropacs.com redirects to braintreehealth.com), suggesting origins in or strong ties to nephrology/vascular access imaging.\n     9→\n    10→Braintree has two business divisions: **Braintree Products** (direct software sales to healthcare facilities in the US and Canada) and **Braintree Operations** (IT and billing management services for outpatient procedure centers). This dual model means the company both sells software and operates as a managed services provider for billing and IT. The contact listed on the CHPL certification is Sunil Reddy, and the support email uses the domain braintreemd.com. The company appears to be a small/niche vendor — no employee count or customer numbers are published, and no third-party reviews were found on G2, Capterra, or Software Advice (search results for \"Braintree\" on these sites return only PayPal Braintree, an unrelated payment processor).\n    11→\n    12→## Product: BRAINTREE (BMW Platform)\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 10727\n    15→Version: 10.5.1.1\n    16→Certification Date: 2021-11-19\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→The BRAINTREE product is the BMW (Braintree Medical Workflow) Platform — described as \"an intranet, web-based medical workflow software application that combines all the functions of every department in a medical facility into one complete package.\" It is a broadly certified ambulatory EHR with 49 certified criteria spanning clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(15), transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1)-(e)(3), public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(7), FHIR API (g)(10), and clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(4). The SED description states it \"supports a variety of healthcare professional roles (e.g., Nurses, Medical Assistants, Physicians, etc.) across a variety of healthcare practices (e.g., Family Practice, Vascular, Interventional Radiology, etc.).\"\n    21→\n    22→Unlike many ambulatory EHRs that focus purely on clinical documentation, the BMW Platform is distinguished by its deep integration of imaging (PACS/RIS), inventory management, and procedure-oriented workflow — reflecting its core market in outpatient procedure and surgery centers rather than primary care offices.\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→**Target settings:**\n    27→- Vascular access centers (appears to be the primary/origin market)\n    28→- Ambulatory surgery centers\n    29→- Outpatient procedure centers\n    30→- Interventional radiology centers\n    31→- Pain management centers\n    32→- Imaging/cath labs\n    33→- Orthopedic centers\n    34→- Behavioral health clinics\n    35→- Hospitals (marketed but unclear how many hospital deployments exist)\n    36→\n    37→**End users:** Physicians, nurses, medical assistants, billing staff, supply clerks, receptionists, and practice managers. The system is designed to serve every department in a medical facility.\n    38→\n    39→**Market size:** Unknown. No customer counts, user numbers, or notable customer references are published on the website. No third-party reviews were found. The company is likely a small niche vendor serving a limited number of procedure-oriented facilities. The dual \"Products\" and \"Operations\" business model suggests a hands-on, managed-services relationship with customers rather than large-scale software distribution.\n    40→\n    41→**Geography:** US and Canada (per vendor website).\n    42→\n    43→### Modules & Functionality\n    44→\n    45→The BMW Platform consists of five integrated modules plus supporting capabilities:\n    46→\n    47→**BT Scheduler** — Appointment scheduling with electronic calendar, multi-doctor and multi-hospital scheduling support, daily/weekly/monthly views, patient admission workflow integration. Links to EMR and imaging worklists.\n    48→\n    49→**BT RIS (Radiology Information System)** — Web-based database for radiology procedure information. Manages patient scheduling, procedure tracking, results reporting, and film tracking. Integrates with BT PACS for image management.\n    50→\n    51→**BT PACS (Picture Archive Communication System)** — Medical imaging management with FDA-approved viewer. Handles image acquisition, archiving, compression/backup, display, and routing. DICOM-compliant. Images attach to patient EMR records. Eliminates need for physical film storage.\n    52→\n    53→**BT Inventory** — Real-time supply and inventory management. Tracks per-procedure supply consumption, stock levels, low-stock alerts, purchase order generation, and spending analytics. Nursing staff log supplies used during procedures, which are automatically deducted from master inventory.\n    54→\n    55→**BT Monitor** — System and infrastructure monitoring tool. Tracks EMR/practice management server health, network infrastructure, disk/memory/CPU usage, facility environmental conditions (temperature). Sends alerts via email, text, and instant message. Archives system logs.\n    56→\n    57→**EMR/Clinical Documentation** — Pre-operative, intra-operative, and post-operative clinical documentation. Point-and-click workflows. Vital signs capture from monitors. Medication management with e-prescribing (additional per-provider fee for e-prescription license). Nursing documentation. Clinical notes and dictation. Drug interaction checking. Problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists. Clinical decision support.\n    58→\n    59→**Practice Analysis/Reporting** — Business analytics covering procedure volumes vs. charges, referral physician trends, staff productivity, wait times, receivables/payment cycles, and quality outcomes. Chart audits and recertification tracking.\n    60→\n    61→**Billing** — Integrated billing with CPT code auto-encoding during procedures. When nurses record procedural steps, billing codes are simultaneously generated. Billing can be completed same-day because encoding is done during the procedure. (Note: Braintree Operations also offers billing management as a service.)\n    62→\n    63→**Patient Portal** — Certified for (e)(1) patient access. Patient portal mentioned on the website along with referral portal for physicians.\n    64→\n    65→**Referral Portal** — Enables referring physicians to access patient DICOM images, clinical cines, and reports in real-time.\n    66→\n    67→**Interoperability** — HL7 interfaces, DICOM worklist support, health information exchange. Interfaces mentioned with Transonic and Vasc-Alert (vascular access monitoring devices). FHIR API (g)(10) certified.\n    68→\n    69→**Public Health Reporting** — Certified for immunization registries (f)(1)), syndromic surveillance (f)(2), electronic lab reporting (f)(3), cancer case reporting (f)(5), transmission to public health agencies (f)(6)-(f)(7).\n    70→\n    71→**Compliance/Quality** — Meaningful Use checklists, MACRA/MIPS reporting, clinical quality measures (CQMs).\n The company develops the Braintree Medical Workflow (BMW) Platform, an integrated software suite designed primarily for outpatient procedure centers, ambulatory surgery centers, vascular access centers, and specialty clinics. The company also operates under the name \"NephroPACS\" (nephropacs.com redirects to braintreehealth.com), suggesting origins in or strong ties to nephrology/vascular access imaging.\n\nBraintree has two business divisions: **Braintree Products** (direct software sales to healthcare facilities in the US and Canada) and **Braintree Operations** (IT and billing management services for outpatient procedure centers). This dual model means the company both sells software and operates as a managed services provider for billing and IT. The contact listed on the CHPL certification is Sunil Reddy, and the support email uses the domain braintreemd.com. The company appears to be a small/niche vendor — no employee count or customer numbers are published, and no third-party reviews were found on G2, Capterra, or Software Advice (search results for \"Braintree\" on these sites return only PayPal Braintree, an unrelated payment processor).\n\n## Product: BRAINTREE (BMW Platform)\n\nCHPL ID: 10727\nVersion: 10.5.1.1\nCertification Date: 2021-11-19\n\n### What It Is\n\nThe BRAINTREE product is the BMW (Braintree Medical Workflow) Platform — described as \"an intranet, web-based medical workflow software application that combines all the functions of every department in a medical facility into one complete package.\" It is a broadly certified ambulatory EHR with 49 certified criteria spanning clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(15), transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1)-(e)(3), public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(7), FHIR API (g)(10), and clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(4). The SED description states it \"supports a variety of healthcare professional roles (e.g., Nurses, Medical Assistants, Physicians, etc.) across a variety of healthcare practices (e.g., Family Practice, Vascular, Interventional Radiology, etc.).\"\n\nUnlike many ambulatory EHRs that focus purely on clinical documentation, the BMW Platform is distinguished by its deep integration of imaging (PACS/RIS), inventory management, and procedure-oriented workflow — reflecting its core market in outpatient procedure and surgery centers rather than primary care offices.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target settings:**\n- Vascular access centers (appears to be the primary/origin market)\n- Ambulatory surgery centers\n- Outpatient procedure centers\n- Interventional radiology centers\n- Pain management centers\n- Imaging/cath labs\n- Orthopedic centers\n- Behavioral health clinics\n- Hospitals (marketed but unclear how many hospital deployments exist)\n\n**End users:** Physicians, nurses, medical assistants, billing staff, supply clerks, receptionists, and practice managers. The system is designed to serve every department in a medical facility.\n\n**Market size:** Unknown. No customer counts, user numbers, or notable customer references are published on the website. No third-party reviews were found. The company is likely a small niche vendor serving a limited number of procedure-oriented facilities. The dual \"Products\" and \"Operations\" business model suggests a hands-on, managed-services relationship with customers rather than large-scale software distribution.\n\n**Geography:** US and Canada (per vendor website).\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nThe BMW Platform consists of five integrated modules plus supporting capabilities:\n\n**BT Scheduler** — Appointment scheduling with electronic calendar, multi-doctor and multi-hospital scheduling support, daily/weekly/monthly views, patient admission workflow integration. Links to EMR and imaging worklists.\n\n**BT RIS (Radiology Information System)** — Web-based database for radiology procedure information. Manages patient scheduling, procedure tracking, results reporting, and film tracking. Integrates with BT PACS for image management.\n\n**BT PACS (Picture Archive Communication System)** — Medical imaging management with FDA-approved viewer. Handles image acquisition, archiving, compression/backup, display, and routing. DICOM-compliant. Images attach to patient EMR records. Eliminates need for physical film storage.\n\n**BT Inventory** — Real-time supply and inventory management. Tracks per-procedure supply consumption, stock levels, low-stock alerts, purchase order generation, and spending analytics. Nursing staff log supplies used during procedures, which are automatically deducted from master inventory.\n\n**BT Monitor** — System and infrastructure monitoring tool. Tracks EMR/practice management server health, network infrastructure, disk/memory/CPU usage, facility environmental conditions (temperature). Sends alerts via email, text, and instant message. Archives system logs.\n\n**EMR/Clinical Documentation** — Pre-operative, intra-operative, and post-operative clinical documentation. Point-and-click workflows. Vital signs capture from monitors. Medication management with e-prescribing (additional per-provider fee for e-prescription license). Nursing documentation. Clinical notes and dictation. Drug interaction checking. Problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists. Clinical decision support.\n\n**Practice Analysis/Reporting** — Business analytics covering procedure volumes vs. charges, referral physician trends, staff productivity, wait times, receivables/payment cycles, and quality outcomes. Chart audits and recertification tracking.\n\n**Billing** — Integrated billing with CPT code auto-encoding during procedures. When nurses record procedural steps, billing codes are simultaneously generated. Billing can be completed same-day because encoding is done during the procedure. (Note: Braintree Operations also offers billing management as a service.)\n\n**Patient Portal** — Certified for (e)(1) patient access. Patient portal mentioned on the website along with referral portal for physicians.\n\n**Referral Portal** — Enables referring physicians to access patient DICOM images, clinical cines, and reports in real-time.\n\n**Interoperability** — HL7 interfaces, DICOM worklist support, health information exchange. Interfaces mentioned with Transonic and Vasc-Alert (vascular access monitoring devices). FHIR API (g)(10) certified.\n\n**Public Health Reporting** — Certified for immunization registries (f)(1)), syndromic surveillance (f)(2), electronic lab reporting (f)(3), cancer case reporting (f)(5), transmission to public health agencies (f)(6)-(f)(7).\n\n**Compliance/Quality** — Meaningful Use checklists, MACRA/MIPS reporting, clinical quality measures (CQMs).\n\n**Population Health** — Website mentions MSO/ACO and chronic care management workflows, though details are sparse.\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the vendor's product descriptions and the typical workflow scenario Braintree (10.5.1.1)\nINTRODUCTION\n\nIn this documentation we will list all the steps required to access protected health information based on the documentation of an open API. The API\nmakes health information broadly available using FHIR®, a set of clinical interoperability resources under the umbrella of the HL7 standards\norganization. FHIR is based on common web standards can be reached through a RESTful protocol in which each FHIR resource has a known URL.\nThis document describes OAuth 2.0 for client applications to authorize, authenticate, and integrate with FHIR-based data systems.\nWe follow Smart-On-FHIR authentication (OAuth2) and authorization (Ref. http://hl7.org/fhir/smart-app-launch/toc.html )\n\nFHIR Endpoints\nNote: Production and Test Server endpoints are subject to change.\n\nProduction Endpoint\nFHIR : https://fhirserver.braintreemd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/\nAuthentication Server: https://fhiroaserver.braintreemd.com/\n\nTest Server Endpoint\nFHIR : https://fhirserver.braintreemd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/\nAuthentication Server: https://fhiroaserver.braintreemd.com/\n\nTypes of Smart-On-FHIR applications\nStandalone App : SMART on FHIR confidential client with a patient context, refresh token, and OpenID Connect (OIDC)\nidentity token. EHR Embedded app: Demonstrate the ability to perform an EHR launch to a SMART on FHIR\nSmart Backend Services App (Multi-patient authorization and API) : These are server-to-server backend applications e.g. Export clinical data for\nmultiple patients in a group. This app is system level app without any UI.\n\nTypes of Authentications supported\nSymmetric (“client secret”) authentication (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Symmetric Client Auth - FHIR v4.0.1)\nAsymmetric (“private key JWT”) authentication (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Asymmetric Client Auth FHIR v4.0.1) Public Clients (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Public Client - FHIR v4.0.1)\n\nSteps for Smart App Launch\n1.\n\nClient App Registration\n\n2.\n\nRetrieve .well-known/smart-configuration\n\n3.\n4.\n\nObtain authorization code\nObtain access token\n\n5.\n\nAccess FHIR API\n\n6.\n\nRefresh Token to obtain new access token\n\nClient APP Registration\n\nWWW.BRAINTREEHEALTH.\n\n\fFHIR\nBefore a SMART app can run against an EHR, the app must be registered with that EHR’s authorization service. We are using OAuth 2.0 Client Registration.\n\nClient App Request process\nThird-party application must sign the API Subscription Agreement with following details\no\no\no\no\no\n\nThe app name\nThe necessary APIs / Scopes\nAny redirect URIs\nLaunch_URL (optional)\nURL to JWK Set (Only for apps supporting asymmetric client authentication).\n\nThe application is created by the EHR admin after confirming the app’s registration parameters and communicates a client_id to the app.\no FHIR APIs (of any supported version) listed within the USCDI v1 core data set will be supported\no Only reads data from FHIR server\n\nRetrieve .well-known/smart-configuration\nIn order to obtain launch context and request authorization to access FHIR resources, the app discovers the EHR FHIR server’s SMART configuration\nmetadata, including OAuth authorization_endpoint and token_endpoint URLs.\nFHIR server makes SMART configuration available from well-known endpoint. You can get Authorization end point and token endpoint.\n\nRequest\nGET 200 https://fhirserver.braintreemd.com/r4/.well-known/smart-configuration\n\nResponse\n{\n\"capabilities\": [\n\"launch-ehr\",\n\"launchstandalone\",\n\"client-public\",\n\"client-confidentialsymmetric\", \"sso-openidconnect\",\n\"context-banner\",\n\"context-style\",\n\"context-ehr-patient\",\n\"context-ehr-encounter\",\n\"context-standalone-patient\",\n\"context-standaloneencounter\", \"permissionoffline\", \"permission-patient\",\n\"permission-user\"\n],\n\"revocation_endpoint\": \"https://fhiroaserver.braintreemd.com/outh/token/revoketoken\", \"authorization_endpoint\":\n\"https://fhiroaserver.braintreemd.com/outh/authorization\", \"token_endpoint\":\n\"https://fhiroaserver.braintreemd.com/outh/token\"\n}\n\nError codes\nJSON response is returned from the FHIR Server along with\nthe code 200: Ok\n401: Unauthorized\n400: Bad Request\n500: Internal Server Error\n\nWWW.BRAINTREEHEALTH.\n\n\fFHIR\n\nObtain authorization code\nThe app supplies the following parameters to the EHR’s “authorize” endpoint.\n\nParameters\n\nresponse_type\n\nrequired\n\nFixed value: code.\n\nclient_id\n\nrequired\n\nThe client's identifier.\n\nredirect_uri\n\nrequired\n\nMust match one of the client's pre-registered redirect URIs.\n\nlaunch\n\nconditional\n\nWhen using the EHR Laun flow, this must match the launch value received from the EHR. Omitted when using the Stand\n\nscope\n\nrequired\n\nMust describe the access that the app needs, including scopes like patient/*.rs, openid and fhirUser (if app needs authenticated\npatient identity) and either:\n\n.\n\na launch value indicating that the app wants to receive already-established launch context details from the EHR\na set of launch context requirements in the form launch/patient, which asks the EHR to establish context on your\nbehalf. OperationOutcome explains the error reported\n\nProfile audience and scope\nThis profile is intended to be used by developers of apps that need to access user identity information or other FHIR resources by requesting\nauthorization from OAuth 2.0 compliant authorization servers.\nThe profile defines a method through which an app requests authorization to access a FHIR resource, and then uses that authorization to retrieve the\nresource. Synchronization of patient context is not addressed; for use cases that require context synchronization (e.g., learning about when the incontext patient changes within an EHR session) In other words, if the patient chart is changed during the session, the application will not inherently be\nupdated.\n\nSecurity and Privacy Considerations\nApp Protection\nThe app is responsible for protecting itself from potential misbehaving or malicious values passed to its redirect URL (e.g., values injected with\nexecutable code, such as SQL) and for protecting authorization codes, access tokens, and refresh tokens from unauthorized access and use. The app\ndeveloper must be aware of potential threats, such as malicious apps running on the same platform, counterfeit authorization servers, and counterfeit\nresource servers, and implement countermeasures to help protect both the app itself and any sensitive information it may hold. For background, see\nthe OAuth 2.0 Threat Model and Security Considerations.\nSpecific requirements are:\nApps SHALL ensure that sensitive information (authentication secrets, authorization codes, tokens) is transmitted ONLY to authenticated\nservers, over TLS- secured channels.\nApps SHALL generate an unpredictable state parameter for each user session; SHALL include state with all authorization requests; and\nSHALL validate the state value for any request sent to its redirect URL.\nAn app SHALL NOT execute untrusted user-supplied inputs as code.\nApp SHALL NOT forward values passed back to its redirect URL to any other arbitrary or user-provided URL (a practice known as an “open\nredirector”). An app SHALL NOT store bearer tokens in cookies that are transmitted as clear text.\nApps SHOULD persist tokens and other sensitive data in app-specific storage locations only, and SHOULD NOT persist them in system-widediscoverable locations.\n\nWWW.BRAINTREEHEALTH.\n\n\fFHIR\n\nTerms of Use\n1.\n\n2.\n\n3.\n\nTERMS ACCEPTANCE AND REPRESENTATION\na. All content is statically rendered. The page structure is:\n\n1. **Certification disclosures** — product name, version, certification number, full criteria list\n2. **FHIR g(10) section** — two links:\n   - \"FHIR API DOCUMENTATION\" → `Braintree-FHIR-API-Documentation-1.pdf` (76 pages, uploaded Jan 2026)\n   - \"FHIR BASE URL\" → `Braintree-fhir-doc.pdf` (1 page, uploaded Nov 2022) — lists test and production FHIR endpoints\n3. **Electronic Health Information export b(10) section** — a short paragraph with a bullet list describing export formats. No linked documents, no data dictionary, no downloadable artifacts.\n4. **CQMs table**, additional software, costs, accessibility, RWT plans/results\n\nThe b(10) EHI export section contains only inline text — there are no linked PDFs, data dictionaries, schemas, or downloadable files specific to the EHI export. The entire b(10) documentation is six bullet points on the page.\n\n```bash\n# Extracted all downloadable file links from the page:\ngrep -oiE 'href=\"[^\"]*\\.(pdf|zip|xlsx|csv|json|doc)[^\"]*\"' /tmp/braintree-page.html\n# Found 11 PDFs — 2 FHIR-related, 1 API TOU, 1 Access Application, 6 RWT plans/results, 1 transparency spreadsheet\n# No EHI-export-specific downloadable files exist.\n```\n\nDownloaded:\n- `Braintree-FHIR-API-Documentation-1.pdf` — the g(10) FHIR API documentation\n- `Braintree-fhir-doc.pdf` — FHIR server endpoint URLs\n- `transparency.htm` — cost disclosure (Excel-exported HTML spreadsheet)\n- `certification-page.html` — saved copy of the full certification page\n- Screenshots of the full page and the b(10) section\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### EHI Export b(10) Documentation\n\nThe entire b(10) EHI export documentation consists of a brief paragraph and six bullet points on the certification page, under the heading \"Electronic Health Information export b(10)\":\n\n> Braintree understands the importance of ensuring patients have timely, secure and easy access to electronic health information (\"EHI\") to empower them in managing their own health and well-being.\n>\n> Key Information About the Exported Data:\n> - While certain EHI, such as images, documents, reports are exported in human-readable html/pdf format where applicable.\n> - The patient demographics are exported in CCDA xml format.\n> - The consents form of the patient are exported in HTML format.\n> - The clinical and billing report and encounter documentation of the patient is exported in PDF format. The PDF provided are in computable format.\n> - The patient attachments are exported in the original format as uploaded into EMR.\n> - The patient images are exported into DICOM format.\n\nThat is the complete documentation. There is no linked data dictionary, no schema, no sample export, no user guide, and no detailed description of what data is included in the export.\n\n### FHIR g(10) API Documentation\n\nThe 76-page FHIR API documentation PDF (uploaded January 2026) is a standard SMART on FHIR API document covering:\n- SMART app launch flow (OAuth2 authorization/authentication)\n- FHIR endpoints (production and test server URLs)\n- Supported FHIR resources: Patient, AllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition (Problems/Health Concerns), Device (Implantable), DiagnosticReport/Clinical Notes/DocumentReference, Observation (Lab Results), Goal, Immunization, Medication/MedicationRequest, Observation (Smoking Status), Procedure, Provenance, Observation (Vital Signs)\n- Sample request/response JSON for each resource\n- Terms of Use\n\nThis is purely g(10) FHIR API documentation — it covers only US Core / USCDI v1 data classes. It does not mention the b(10) EHI export at all.\n\n### FHIR Base URL Document\n\nA single-page PDF listing four endpoints:\n- Test FHIR Server: `https://fhirserver.braintreemd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/`\n- Test Auth Server: `https://fhiroaserver.braintreemd.com`\n- Production FHIR Server: `https://btfhir.braintreemd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/`\n- Production Auth Server: `https://btoaserver.braintreemd.com`\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nBased on the product research, Braintree's BMW Platform stores a wide range of data: clinical documentation (pre-op, intra-op, post-op), medical imaging (PACS/DICOM), radiology information (RIS), billing with auto-CPT encoding, inventory/supply chain, scheduling, and specialty data for vascular access, interventional radiology, and ambulatory surgery centers.\n\nThe b(10) export documentation describes these output formats:\n- **Demographics** — CCDA XML (covers basic patient demographics)\n- **Consent forms** — HTML format\n- **Clinical and billing reports, encounter documentation** — PDF format (described as \"computable format\")\n- **Attachments** — original upload format\n- **Medical images** — DICOM format\n\n**Domains that appear covered (at least partially):**\n- Patient demographics (via CCDA)\n- Clinical notes and encounter documentation (via PDF)\n- Billing reports (via PDF — though scope unclear)\n- Medical images (via DICOM — appropriate for this imaging-heavy product)\n- Patient attachments (original format)\n- Consent forms (HTML)\n\n**Domains with no mention or unclear coverage:**\n- **Procedure-specific documentation** (pre-op, intra-op, post-op workflows) — may be included in \"encounter documentation\" PDFs but not specified\n- **Medications and prescriptions** — not mentioned in b(10) export\n- **Lab results and diagnostic reports** — not mentioned\n- **Allergies** — not mentioned\n- **Problem lists / diagnoses** — not mentioned\n- **Vital signs** — not mentioned\n- **Immunization records** — not mentioned\n- **Radiology reports** (RIS data) — unclear if included in PDFs or separate\n- **Scheduling/appointment data** — not mentioned\n- **Inventory/supply consumption per procedure** — not EHI (operational), so correctly absent\n- **Detailed billing data** (CPT codes, charges, payment details) — \"billing report\" is mentioned but no detail on granularity\n- **Referral data** — not mentioned\n- **Care plans** — not mentioned\n\nThe export claims to include \"clinical and billing report and encounter documentation\" in PDF format, which could conceivably be comprehensive — but without a data dictionary or detailed description, it's impossible to verify what data fields are actually included. The claim that PDFs are in \"computable format\" is notable but unexplained.\n\n### The b(10) vs g(10) Question\n\nNotably, Braintree does **not** conflate their b(10) and g(10) exports — they are presented as distinct sections on the page. The b(10) export uses CCDA, HTML, PDF, and DICOM formats rather than the FHIR API. This suggests the vendor has made some effort to distinguish the two requirements.\n\nHowever, the b(10) documentation is so sparse that it's impossible to assess whether the export actually covers \"all electronic health information\" or just a subset. The g(10) FHIR API covers only standard US Core resources, and the b(10) section doesn't reference FHIR at all — which is actually appropriate for a procedure-center product with imaging, billing, and specialty data that doesn't map neatly to FHIR US Core.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a mix of formats:\n- **CCDA XML** for demographics — a recognized standard, though using CCDA just for demographics seems like an odd choice when the rest of the export is non-FHIR\n- **PDF** for clinical and billing documentation — described as \"computable format,\" which likely means structured/tagged PDFs rather than scanned images. However, PDF is inherently limited for data portability compared to structured formats like CSV, JSON, or XML\n- **HTML** for consent forms — readable but not ideal for data interchange\n- **DICOM** for medical images — the correct standard for imaging data\n- **Original format** for attachments — preserves fidelity but depends on what was uploaded\n\nThe format choices are reasonable for human readability but raise concerns about machine-processability. PDFs — even \"computable\" ones — make it difficult for a receiving system to import and reconstruct structured data. A third party would be able to view the records but would struggle to programmatically ingest them.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThe b(10) export documentation quality is **very poor**:\n- No data dictionary whatsoever — no tables, fields, schemas, or column definitions\n- No sample export files or examples\n- No user guide or instructions for performing the export\n- No description of the export process (how it's triggered, who can request it, what parameters exist)\n- No enumeration of what specific data elements are included\n- No versioning or change history\n- The six bullet points read more like a compliance checkbox than genuine technical documentation\n\nThe claim that \"PDFs are in computable format\" is the only semi-technical detail provided, and it's not explained. Does this mean PDF/A? Tagged PDFs? PDF with embedded structured data?\n\nA developer tasked with importing data from this export would have almost nothing to work with. They would need to receive an actual export file to reverse-engineer the structure.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Extremely low. No field-level documentation exists. The documentation operates at the level of \"demographics are in CCDA\" and \"billing is in PDF\" — there is no description of which demographic fields, which billing elements, or what constitutes an \"encounter documentation\" PDF.\n- **Coded fields**: Not documented. No value sets, no code systems referenced.\n- **Relationships**: Not documented. No description of how the various exported files relate to each other or how a receiving system would correlate them.\n- **Completeness signal**: The absence of any mention of medications, allergies, labs, vitals, immunizations, problem lists, or care plans in the b(10) section is concerning — these are core clinical data domains that the product stores (and that are available via the g(10) FHIR API). It's unclear whether they're included in the PDF \"encounter documentation\" or genuinely missing from the export.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nBraintree Health has done the minimum to acknowledge the b(10) requirement — they have a labeled section on their certification page that describes export formats at a very high level. The fact that the b(10) export uses non-FHIR formats (CCDA, PDF, DICOM) and is presented separately from the g(10) API suggests some genuine effort to distinguish the requirements.\n\nHowever, the documentation is far too sparse to assess whether the export actually covers all EHI. For a product that stores procedure-specific clinical workflows, medical imaging, radiology information, billing with auto-CPT encoding, and inventory data, six bullet points is inadequate documentation. The most critical missing piece is a data dictionary or detailed listing of what data elements the export includes across the product's various modules (BT Scheduler, BT RIS, BT PACS, BT Inventory, EMR, Billing).\n\nThe use of PDF as the primary format for clinical and billing data, while described as \"computable,\" is a significant limitation for data portability. The DICOM export for images is appropriate and is actually a strength — this is the correct format for the imaging data that is central to this product's use case.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL: https://www.braintreehealth.com/braintree-onc-certification-btree/ (no redirects)\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, all content visible without JavaScript)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (b(10) section is inline on the page, no clicks needed)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- No obstacles encountered. The page loaded cleanly with curl.\n- The transparency.htm file is an Excel-exported HTML frameset for cost disclosures — not relevant to the EHI export.\n- No EHI-export-specific downloadable artifacts exist on the page; the b(10) documentation is entirely inline text.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":442,"cache_read_input_tokens":89374,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":442},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6cde0cb3-de06-4ae6-b391-910e3a782d9b","uuid":"1d0400c1-71c1-48a0-bea6-ce7059d8d5cf"}

It's unclear whether they're included in the PDF \"encounter documentation\" or genuinely missing from the export.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nBraintree Health has done the minimum to acknowledge the b(10) requirement — they have a labeled section on their certification page that describes export formats at a very high level. The fact that the b(10) export uses non-FHIR formats (CCDA, PDF, DICOM) and is presented separately from the g(10) API suggests some genuine effort to distinguish the requirements.\n\nHowever, the documentation is far too sparse to assess whether the export actually covers all EHI. For a product that stores procedure-specific clinical workflows, medical imaging, radiology information, billing with auto-CPT encoding, and inventory data, six bullet points is inadequate documentation. The most critical missing piece is a data dictionary or detailed listing of what data elements the export includes across the product's various modules (BT Scheduler, BT RIS, BT PACS, BT Inventory, EMR, Billing).\n\nThe use of PDF as the primary format for clinical and billing data, while described as \"computable,\" is a significant limitation for data portability. The DICOM export for images is appropriate and is actually a strength — this is the correct format for the imaging data that is central to this product's use case.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL: https://www.braintreehealth.com/braintree-onc-certification-btree/ (no redirects)\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, all content visible without JavaScript)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (b(10) section is inline on the page, no clicks needed)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- No obstacles encountered.
